不断上升的生物安全威胁:昆士兰州推进应对准备的方法的见解

Rising biosecurity threats: Insights from Queensland’s approach to advancing response readiness
作者： Rachel Chay, Michael Reid, Marni Manning
摘要中文翻译有效的生物安全是一项日益严峻的全球挑战,其规模、复杂性、成本和后果迅速升级。最佳实践生物安全旨在保护人类、动物和环境免受外来病虫害的侵害,但确保有弹性的应对框架仍然很困难,即使在澳大利亚等地理位置偏远的国家也是如此。生物安全事件是一种被低估的安全风险,在多重危机时代,其频率和强度都在增加,气候变化、自然灾害、地缘政治不稳定、供应链中断、粮食不安全、流行病和经济波动相互作用并放大影响。本文从政府从业者的角度出发,认为生物安全必须被理解为一个抗灾问题,而不仅仅是一个农业问题。它以红火蚁和 高致病性禽流感 的防备为例,强调了生物威胁如何与公共卫生、环境管理、应急准备和社区复原力日益交叉。该文件论证了早期发现、跨部门协调、激增能力、机构适应性和持续社区参与的重要性。行业、政府和社区在预防、识别和应对生物安全威胁方面都发挥着关键作用。推进应对准备工作需要更强有力的伙伴关系、可信网络和治理安排,以便能够在日益复杂、相互关联和不确定的运营环境中管理不断升级的生物风险。本文还包含在《商业与管理合集》中,可通过 https://hstalks.com/business/ 访问。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注有效生物安全方面日益严峻的全球挑战,特别是在 禽流感 A 病毒、禽源人畜共患感染及其亚型的背景下。核心问题是,考虑到在多重危机时代这些生物威胁的频率和强度不断增加,如何加强对这些生物威胁的应对准备。范围包括将生物安全视为一个抗灾问题,而不仅仅是农业问题,并强调跨部门协调、早期发现和社区参与的必要性。
证据基础与方法
该审查基于政府从业者的角度,借鉴了红火蚁和 高致病性禽流感 的准备等例子。它不报告特定数据库、检索日期或资格标准。方法部分强调了早期发现、跨部门协调、激增能力、机构适应性和持续社区参与作为弹性生物安全框架关键组成部分的重要性。
共识与结论
生物安全事件是一种被低估的安全风险,由于多种全球危机的相互作用,其频率和强度都在增加。它强调生物威胁日益与公共卫生、环境管理、应急准备和社区复原力相互交叉。该文件论证了早期发现、跨部门协调、激增能力、机构适应性和持续社区参与在管理这些风险方面的重要性。
争议与缺口
该审查指出,生物安全缺乏作为抗灾问题而不是农业问题的重视。报告还指出,虽然早期发现和跨部门协调的重要性已得到承认,但在理解如何在日益复杂和相互关联的运营环境中有效实施这些策略方面存在差距。此外,该审查没有提供具体干预措施的有效性或其在不同地区的应用程度的详细证据。
科研与实践启示
审查表明,推进应对准备工作需要更强有力的伙伴关系、值得信赖的网络和能够管理不断升级的生物风险的治理安排。它强调了持续社区参与和机构适应性的必要性。此外,它呼吁采取更加综合的生物安全方法,考虑其与公共卫生、环境管理和应急准备的交叉点。

Rising biosecurity threats: Insights from Queensland’s approach to advancing response readiness

Authors: Rachel Chay, Michael Reid, Marni Manning
Original abstract (English)
Effective biosecurity is a growing global challenge, rapidly escalating in scale, complexity, cost, and consequence. Best-practice biosecurity seeks to protect people, animals, and the environment from exotic pests and diseases, yet ensuring a resilient response framework remains difficult, even in geographically remote countries such as Australia. Biosecurity incidents are an underappreciated security risk, increasing in both frequency and intensity within an era of polycrisis, where climate variability, natural disasters, geopolitical instability, disrupted supply chains, food insecurity, pandemics, and economic volatility interact and amplify impacts. Drawing on a government practitioner perspective, this paper argues that biosecurity must be understood as a disaster resilience issue rather than solely an agricultural one. Using examples including red imported fire ants and preparedness for highly pathogenic avian influenza, it highlights how biological threats increasingly intersect with public health, environmental management, emergency preparedness, and community resilience. The paper demonstrates the importance of early detection, cross-sector coordination, surge capability, institutional adaptability, and sustained community engagement. Industries, governments, and communities all play critical roles in preventing, identifying, and responding to biosecurity threats. Advancing response readiness requires stronger partnerships, trusted networks, and governance arrangements capable of managing escalating biological risks in an increasingly complex, interconnected, and uncertain operating environment. This article is also included in The Business & Management Collection which can be accessed at https://hstalks.com/business/.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the growing global challenge of effective biosecurity, particularly in the context of avian influenza A viruses, avian-origin zoonotic infections, and their subtypes. The central question is how to enhance response readiness to these biological threats, considering their increasing frequency and intensity in an era of polycrisis. The scope includes examining biosecurity as a disaster resilience issue, not solely an agricultural one, and highlights the need for cross-sector coordination, early detection, and community engagement.
Evidence base and method
The review is based on a government practitioner perspective, drawing on examples such as red imported fire ants and preparedness for highly pathogenic avian influenza. It does not report on specific databases, search dates, or eligibility criteria. The methods section emphasizes the importance of early detection, cross-sector coordination, surge capability, institutional adaptability, and sustained community engagement as key components of a resilient biosecurity framework.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that biosecurity incidents are an underappreciated security risk, increasing in both frequency and intensity due to the interactions of multiple global crises. It highlights that biological threats increasingly intersect with public health, environmental management, emergency preparedness, and community resilience. The paper demonstrates the importance of early detection, cross-sector coordination, surge capability, institutional adaptability, and sustained community engagement in managing these risks.
Controversies and gaps
The review identifies a lack of emphasis on biosecurity as a disaster resilience issue rather than an agricultural one. It also notes that while the importance of early detection and cross-sector coordination is acknowledged, there is a gap in understanding how these strategies can be effectively implemented in an increasingly complex and interconnected operating environment. Additionally, the review does not provide detailed evidence on the effectiveness of specific interventions or the extent of their application across different regions.
Research and practice implications
The review suggests that advancing response readiness requires stronger partnerships, trusted networks, and governance arrangements capable of managing escalating biological risks. It highlights the need for sustained community engagement and institutional adaptability. Furthermore, it calls for a more integrated approach to biosecurity that considers its intersection with public health, environmental management, and emergency preparedness.

埃塞俄比亚西北部贡德尔市急性呼吸道感染患者甲型流感病毒感染患病率及相关因素

Prevalence and associated factors of influenza A virus infection among patients with acute respiratory tract infections in Gondar City, Northwest Ethiopia

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

禽流感之战:印度新 疫苗 针对家禽中致命的 H5N1

Bird Flu Battle: India's New Vaccine Targets Deadly H5N1 in Poultry
新闻简报据 2026 年 11 月 19 日的新闻文章报道,印度正准备在鸡中推出首个国内开发的针对 H5N1 禽流感 的 疫苗。疫苗 由印度农业研究委员会国家高安全动物疾病研究所 (ICAR-NIHSAD) 的科学家开发,针对该国与 暴发 相关的特定 H5N1 进化枝。虽然 疫苗 预计将减少家禽死亡和病毒传播,但一些研究表明,接种疫苗的家禽仍可能传播病毒,这凸显了继续评估的必要性。自 2006 年以来,政府已报告了 500 多例 H5N1 和 H5N8 暴发 混合病例,强调了这种新工具在控制该疾病方面的重要性。然而,疫苗 的有效性和影响将取决于其实施、监管批准以及与 监测 和生物安全措施的整合。世界卫生组织 (WHO) 指出,人类感染 H5N1 通常与接触受感染的动物有关,并且尚未观察到持续的人际 传播。这一发展是保护家禽生产、保障农民生计和减少危险 禽流感 病毒传播的更广泛战略的一部分。
查看新闻五要素
时间
据 N7 报道,疫苗 将于 2026 年 11 月 20 日上市。发布日期为2026年11月19日(N2)。
地点与对象
印度是重点关注其家禽业的地点,特别是在易受 暴发(N5、N13、N33)反复影响的地区。
事件
印度政府 (N5) 证实,印度正在推出首个自主研发的 疫苗,用于对抗鸡中的 H5N1 禽流感。
规模、影响与风险
自 2006 年以来,印度已报告了 500 多起 H5N1 和 H5N8 暴发 病例 (N12)。疫苗 旨在减少家禽死亡和病毒传播,尽管一些接种疫苗的家禽可能仍会传播病毒(N19、N44)。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 的部署和有效性取决于监管部门的批准、疫苗接种 策略和现场监控(N15、N24)。WHO 指出,尚未证明 H5 病毒在人与人之间存在持续的 传播 (N30)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Bird Flu Battle: India's New Vaccine Targets Deadly H5N1 in Poultry

News Brief
India is preparing to launch its first domestically developed vaccine against H5N1 avian influenza in chickens, as reported in a November 19, 2026, news article. The vaccine, developed by scientists at the Indian Council of Agricultural Research–National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (ICAR-NIHSAD), targets a specific H5N1 clade associated with outbreaks in the country. While the vaccine is expected to reduce poultry deaths and viral shedding, some studies suggest that vaccinated birds may still shed the virus, highlighting the need for continued evaluation. The government has reported over 500 combined H5N1 and H5N8 outbreaks since 2006, underscoring the importance of this new tool in controlling the disease. However, the vaccine's effectiveness and impact will depend on its implementation, regulatory approvals, and integration with surveillance and biosecurity measures. The World Health Organization (WHO) notes that human infections with H5N1 are generally linked to exposure to infected animals, and sustained human-to-human transmission has not been observed. This development is part of a broader strategy to protect poultry production, safeguard farmers' livelihoods, and reduce the circulation of dangerous avian influenza viruses.

MEDINDIA MEDINDIA Consult Online Doctor Courses Explore Healthy Living News Health A-Z Calculators Articles Drugs Directories Education More Search Medindia Medindia » News » Bird Flu News » Bird Flu Battle: India's New Vaccine Targets Deadly H5N1 in Poultry Bird Flu Battle: India's New Vaccine Targets Deadly H5N1 in Poultry Written by Colleen Fleiss Reviewed by The Medindia News Review Team on Nov 19 2026 7:25 PM Add MedIndia on Google Set MedIndia as a preferred source on Google to consistently get trusted, expert-backed health and wellness content in your search results. Can India's new H5N1 chicken vaccine do more than prevent deaths? Discover how vaccine research is tackling bird flu, viral shedding and poultry protection. India is set to introduce its first indigenously developed vaccine against H5N1 avian influenza (bird flu) in chickens, marking an important step toward strengthening poultry disease control, reducing dependence on imported vaccines, and protecting the country's poultry industry from devastating outbreaks. The development comes at a time when highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI), particularly H5N1, remains a major concern globally because of its ability to trigger severe outbreaks in poultry, cause high mortality, disrupt food production, and create significant economic losses for farmers. According to reports, India plans to launch the domestically developed H5N1 chicken vaccine on November 20, 2026, potentially adding a new tool to its bird flu prevention and control strategy.() Bird Flu Bird flu (avian influenza/avian flu) is a disease caused by an influenza virus (H5N1) that primarily affects birds but can infect humans also. How the Vaccine Could Strengthen India's Bird Flu Response The vaccine is an inactivated H5N1 vaccine targeting Clade 2.3.2.1a, () a strain associated with outbreaks in Indian poultry. It was developed by scientists at the Indian Council of Agricultural Research–National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (ICAR-NIHSAD), Bhopal. The significance lies in developing a vaccine suited to virus strains circulating in India. Bird flu outbreaks can lead to large-scale poultry deaths, emergency culling, restrictions on movement and trade, and considerable financial losses for farmers. The Government of India says more than 500 combined H5N1 and H5N8 outbreaks have been reported in the country since 2006. The vaccine could provide an additional layer of protection for poultry, particularly in regions vulnerable to recurring outbreaks. However, the announcement of its development and dedication does not necessarily mean immediate nationwide vaccination. Deployment, regulatory approvals, vaccination strategy, monitoring and field effectiveness will determine how soon it can be used and how much impact it delivers. Bird Flu Outbreak Strikes Japan Poultry Farm, HPAI Confirmed Japan reports HPAI bird flu outbreak in Hokkaido poultry farm affecting 190,000 birds, reinforcing global monitoring of avian influenza and zoonotic risk. How Effective Are H5N1 Vaccines in Preventing Bird Flu in Chickens? Research published in 2026 highlights both the potential and the limitations of poultry vaccination. A September 2026 study examining six commercial H5 vaccines against newer H5N1 and H5N8 strains found that vaccination protected many chickens against death, but some vaccinated birds continued shedding virus. () The researchers emphasized that vaccine matching with circulating strains is critical to reducing transmission—not simply preventing mortality. This is particularly relevant to India's vaccine, which targets a specific H5N1 clade. Another study published in Vaccines in November 2026 reported that an enhanced inactivated H5N1 vaccine formulation improved immune responses and accelerated the reduction of viral shedding in experimentally vaccinated chickens. The findings reinforce the importance of vaccine design, immune response and transmission control when evaluating poultry vaccines. () These studies suggest that India's vaccine will need continued evaluation under field conditions to establish how effectively it prevents infection, reduces virus shedding and protects flocks against circulating viruses. Bird Flu Before Christmas: Should Turkey Lovers Be Worried? Bird flu has been confirmed at a Northumberland farm housing 32,000 free-range hens, raising concerns about further outbreaks and possible Christmas turkey supply disruptions. Can India's H5N1 Chicken Vaccine Help Reduce Bird Flu Risk to Humans? H5N1 is primarily an avian virus, but it can infect humans following exposure to infected birds or contaminated environments. Reducing infections in poultry could help lower the risk of human exposure, especially for poultry workers, farm owners, culling teams and people handling infected birds. The World Health Organization (WHO) says human infections are generally associated with exposure to infected animals, and sustained human-to-human transmission of currently circulating H5 viruses has not been demonstrated. () () A One Health approach involving animal, human and environmental surveillance remains essential. India's indigenous H5N1 vaccine is therefore more than a veterinary research achievement. It could become an important part of the country's strategy to protect poultry production, safeguard farmers' livelihoods and reduce the circulation of dangerous avian influenza viruses. Its success, however, will depend on how effectively vaccination is combined with surveillance, biosecurity, outbreak containment and scientific monitoring. 印度将于明天推出首个针对鸡的本土 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗

India to launch first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens tomorrow
新闻简报印度将于 2026 年 11 月 19 日星期四推出首个针对鸡的本土 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗,该疫苗由位于博帕尔的 ICAR-国家高安全动物疾病研究所 (NIHSAD) 的科学家开发。疫苗 针对 H5N1 毒株,特别是进化枝 2.3.2.1a,自 2006 年以来,该毒株一直是该国大多数家禽 暴发 的罪魁祸首。已报告了 500 多起涉及 H5N1 和 H5N8 亚型的禽流感 暴发,导致大规模扑杀、农民经济损失和家禽贸易中断。疫苗 是一项重大发展,因为之前没有针对该菌株的特定商业 疫苗。然而,部长尚未提供有关其商业可用性、生产安排、定价、疫苗接种 时间表或部署规模的详细信息。疫苗 在保护家禽业方面的有效性将取决于其广泛使用以及与疾病 监测、早期 诊断 和农场级生物安全措施的协调。这标志着加强印度本土兽医疫苗研究和降低其家禽业对高致病性禽流感反复出现的暴发的脆弱性迈出了重要一步。
查看新闻五要素
时间
印度将推出首个本土开发的针对高致病性 H5N1 禽流感病毒的 疫苗,这标志着在保护该国家禽业免受反复出现的 禽流感 暴发 感染方面迈出了重要一步。疫苗 将于 2026 年 11 月 19 日星期四献给国家。
地点与对象
印度,专门针对家禽业,由博帕尔的 ICAR 科学家开发了 疫苗。H5N1 菌株(分支 2.3.2.1a)是该国鸡群中大多数禽流感 暴发 的罪魁祸首。
事件
印度将推出首个针对鸡的本土 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗,由 ICAR-国家高安全动物疾病研究所 (NIHSAD) 科学家开发。根据官方公告,这是已确认的事件。
规模、影响与风险
自 2006 年以来,印度已报告了超过 500 种涉及 H5N1 和 H5N8 亚型的禽流感 暴发。疫苗 意义重大,因为之前没有针对该毒株的特定商业 疫苗。暴发 造成了大规模扑杀、农民的经济损失以及家禽贸易的中断。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗有望增强印度防控暴发的能力。不过,部长尚未具体说明 疫苗 的商业可用性、生产安排、定价、疫苗接种 时间表或部署规模和时间表。其有效性将取决于其部署以及与其他措施的协调。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India to launch first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens tomorrow

News Brief
India is set to launch its first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens on Thursday, November 19, 2026, developed by scientists at the ICAR-National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (NIHSAD) in Bhopal. The vaccine targets the H5N1 strain, specifically Clade 2.3.2.1a, which has been responsible for most poultry outbreaks in the country since 2006. Over 500 bird flu outbreaks involving H5N1 and H5N8 subtypes have been reported, leading to mass culling, financial losses for farmers, and disruptions in the poultry trade. The vaccine is a significant development as no specific commercial vaccine was previously available against this strain. However, the minister has not provided details on its commercial availability, production arrangements, pricing, vaccination schedule, or deployment scale. The effectiveness of the vaccine in protecting the poultry sector will depend on its widespread use and coordination with disease surveillance, early diagnosis, and farm-level biosecurity measures. This marks an important step in strengthening India's indigenous veterinary vaccine research and reducing the vulnerability of its poultry industry to recurring outbreaks of highly pathogenic avian influenza.
Original source text (English)
India to launch first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens tomorrow Developed by ICAR scientists in Bhopal, vaccine targets strain responsible for most poultry outbreaks; over 500 bird flu outbreaks reported in India since 2006 India is set to launch its first indigenously developed vaccine against the highly pathogenic H5N1 bird flu virus, marking a significant step towards protecting the country's poultry industry from recurring avian influenza outbreaks. Union Agriculture Minister Shivraj Singh Chouhan will dedicate the inactivated H5N1 (Clade 2.3.2.1a) vaccine for chickens to the nation on Thursday. Developed by scientists at the Bhopal-based ICAR-National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (NIHSAD), the vaccine is the first of its kind in India specifically targeting the H5N1 virus belonging to Clade 2.3.2.1a, a strain identified as responsible for most bird flu outbreaks in chickens in the country. The development is significant as no specific commercial vaccine was previously available against this particular strain. "The indigenous formulation is expected to strengthen India's capacity to prevent and control outbreaks that have repeatedly resulted in mass culling of poultry, financial losses for farmers and disruptions in the poultry trade," an Agriculture Ministry official said. More than 500 bird flu outbreaks involving the H5N1 and H5N8 subtypes have been reported in India since the disease was first detected in the country in 2006, according to the ministry. Highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI), commonly known as bird flu, is a severe infectious disease affecting poultry. Outbreaks can result in mortality approaching 100 per cent among infected birds, often forcing authorities to undertake large-scale culling and impose restrictions on the movement and trade of poultry products. However, the minister has not specified the vaccine's commercial availability, production arrangements, pricing, vaccination schedule or the scale and timeline of its deployment across poultry farms. Its effectiveness in protecting the poultry sector will ultimately depend on how widely and efficiently it is deployed, alongside disease surveillance, early diagnosis, farm-level biosecurity and coordinated outbreak-response measures. The development marks an important step towards strengthening India's indigenous veterinary vaccine research and reducing the vulnerability of its poultry industry to recurring outbreaks of highly pathogenic avian influenza. Beyond its economic impact on poultry, H5N1 remains a significant global public health concern. According to the World Health Organization (WHO), 1,003 human cases of H5N1 avian influenza, including 479 deaths, had been reported across 25 countries, including India, since 2003
View five news elements
Time
India is set to launch its first indigenously developed vaccine against the highly pathogenic H5N1 bird flu virus, marking a significant step towards protecting the country's poultry industry from recurring avian influenza outbreaks. The vaccine will be dedicated to the nation on Thursday, November 19, 2026.
Location and population
India, specifically targeting the poultry industry, with the vaccine developed by ICAR scientists in Bhopal. The H5N1 strain, Clade 2.3.2.1a, is responsible for most bird flu outbreaks in chickens in the country.
Event
India will launch its first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens, developed by ICAR-National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (NIHSAD) scientists. This is a confirmed event as per the official announcement.
Scale, impact and risk
Over 500 bird flu outbreaks involving H5N1 and H5N8 subtypes have been reported in India since 2006. The vaccine is significant as no specific commercial vaccine was previously available against this strain. The outbreaks have caused mass culling, financial losses for farmers, and disruptions in the poultry trade.
Response, status and uncertainty
The vaccine is expected to strengthen India's capacity to prevent and control outbreaks. However, the minister has not specified the vaccine's commercial availability, production arrangements, pricing, vaccination schedule, or deployment scale and timeline. The effectiveness will depend on its deployment and coordination with other measures.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

印度开发出首个本土 H5N1 疫苗 以加强禽流感预防 - 医疗之声

India Develops First Indigenous H5N1 Vaccine to Strengthen Bird Flu Prevention - Voice of Healthcare
新闻简报印度研发出首个本土H5N1 疫苗,标志着在打击禽流感方面迈出了重要一步。疫苗 由印度农业研究委员会 (ICAR) 和国家高安全动物疾病研究所 (NIHSAD) 创建,针对负责家禽 暴发 的 H5N1 分支 2.3.2.1a 菌株,并在其他动物物种中检测到。这一进展旨在加强国家针对该病毒的防范措施,该病毒已导致鸟类和三例人类病例出现高 死亡率 病例。疫苗 预计将减少经济损失并支持限制动物和人类之间 传播 的努力。该信息由一家知名媒体报道,这一进展弥补了印度疾病控制能力的一个关键差距。
查看新闻五要素
时间
疫苗的开发报告于2026年11月18日,事件日期为开发时间,证据中未明确说明。
地点与对象
印度,专门针对受 H5N1 Clade 2.3.2.1a 菌株影响的家禽和其他动物物种,包括哺乳动物。
事件
印度开发出了第一个针对 H5N1 禽流感 病毒的本土生产的 疫苗,该病毒针对与印度家禽中多种禽流感 暴发 相关的 Clade 2.3.2.1a 菌株。印度农业研究委员会 (ICAR) 和国家高安全动物疾病研究所 (NIHSAD) 报告了这一进展。
规模、影响与风险
H5N1 进化枝 2.3.2.1a 菌株与家禽中的 死亡率 相关,感染禽类中几乎 100% 存在 死亡率。在包括哺乳动物在内的其他动物物种中也发现了这种病毒,并已引起人类感染,已报告三例病例。疫苗 的开发旨在解决该病毒带来的经济和健康风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 有望预防属于目标进化枝的循环 H5N1 病毒株。该开发解决了印度疾病控制准备工作中的一个关键差距,因为目前该国没有特定的市售 疫苗 可以针对这种特定菌株提供保护。消息来源为知名媒体,并经过编辑组核实。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India Develops First Indigenous H5N1 Vaccine to Strengthen Bird Flu Prevention - Voice of Healthcare

News Brief
India has developed its first indigenous H5N1 vaccine, marking a significant step in combating avian influenza. The vaccine, created by the Indian Council of Agricultural Research (ICAR) and the National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (NIHSAD), targets the H5N1 Clade 2.3.2.1a strain responsible for poultry outbreaks and detected in other animal species. This development aims to strengthen national preparedness against the virus, which has caused high mortality in birds and three human cases. The vaccine is expected to reduce economic losses and support efforts to limit transmission between animals and humans. The information is reported by a reputable media outlet, and the development addresses a critical gap in India's disease-control capabilities.

India has developed its first indigenously produced vaccine against the H5N1 avian influenza virus, marking an important step towards strengthening national preparedness against highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI). The inactivated vaccine has been developed by the Indian Council of Agricultural Research (ICAR) and the National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (NIHSAD) to target the H5N1 Clade 2.3.2.1a strain associated with multiple bird flu outbreaks in Indian poultry. The development addresses a critical gap in India's disease-control preparedness, as no specific commercially available vaccine currently provides protection against this particular strain in the country. The vaccine is expected to protect against circulating H5N1 strains belonging to the targeted clade. The virus poses significant challenges to poultry farming, with outbreaks potentially causing nearly 100% mortality among infected birds. Beyond livestock losses, bird flu can disrupt international trade, increase poultry product prices and affect farmers' livelihoods. The 2006 bird flu outbreak in India resulted in estimated direct and indirect economic losses of ₹2,200–3,000 crore. Upcoming Conferences and Events Physical EventNov 26th, 2026 Dental Excellence Awards 2026 The public health implications are also significant. According to the World Health Organization (WHO), approximately 1,003 human H5N1 infections, including 479 deaths, have been reported across 25 countries, including India, since 2003. The targeted Clade 2.3.2.1a strain has been associated with outbreaks in poultry and has also been detected in other animal species, including mammals. Three human cases linked to infections with this strain have also been reported, highlighting the importance of controlling its spread across species. The indigenous vaccine represents a significant development in India's veterinary disease-control capabilities, with the potential to strengthen poultry health protection, reduce economic losses and support broader efforts to limit the transmission of avian influenza between animals and humans. Time
The vaccine development was reported on 2026-11-18, with the event date being the time of the development, which is not explicitly stated in the evidence.
Location and population
India, specifically targeting poultry and other animal species, including mammals, that have been affected by the H5N1 Clade 2.3.2.1a strain.
Event
India has developed its first indigenously produced vaccine against the H5N1 avian influenza virus, which targets the Clade 2.3.2.1a strain associated with multiple bird flu outbreaks in Indian poultry. This development is reported by the Indian Council of Agricultural Research (ICAR) and the National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (NIHSAD).
Scale, impact and risk
The H5N1 Clade 2.3.2.1a strain has been associated with outbreaks in poultry, with nearly 100% mortality among infected birds. It has also been detected in other animal species, including mammals, and has caused human infections, with three cases reported. The development of the vaccine aims to address the economic and health risks posed by the virus.
Response, status and uncertainty
The vaccine is expected to protect against circulating H5N1 strains belonging to the targeted clade. The development addresses a critical gap in India's disease-control preparedness, as no specific commercially available vaccine currently provides protection against this particular strain in the country. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

印度将于 11 月 20 日感染首例本土鸡 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗

India to get first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens on November 20
新闻简报印度将于 2026 年 11 月 20 日推出首个针对鸡 H5N1 禽流感病毒的本土 疫苗,该药物由 ICAR-国家高安全动物疾病研究所 (ICAR-NIHSAD) 开发。疫苗 针对 H5N1 进化枝 2.3.2.1a 菌株,该菌株是该国大多数禽流感 暴发 的罪魁祸首,并且还在其他动物物种和三例人类病例中检测到。农业和农民福利部强调,重大禽流感 暴发 可能使印度 20% 以上的家禽种群面临风险,并使 GDP 增长减少高达 1.5%。自2006年以来,已报道了500多起涉及H5N1和H5N8毒株的暴发组合病毒,造成了重大经济损失。疫苗 旨在遏制鸡群中的感染,保护脆弱的牲畜种群,并防止 传播 传染给其他动物和人类。该事件根据官方声明得到证实,是印度应对日益严重的 H5N1 禽流感威胁的努力的一部分。
查看新闻五要素
时间
印度将于 2026 年 11 月 20 日推出首个针对鸡 H5N1 禽流感病毒的本土 疫苗。疫苗 由 ICAR-NIHSAD 开发,旨在提供针对在印度传播的病毒株的保护。该新闻发布日期为2026年11月17日。
地点与对象
疫苗 的目标是在印度高风险农业地区分销,特别是鸡。H5N1 进化枝 2.3.2.1a 菌株是导致鸡中大多数禽流感 暴发 的原因,农业和农民福利部强调了印度家禽数量和 GDP 增长面临的风险。
事件
印度将于 2026 年 11 月 20 日推出首个针对鸡的本土 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗,由 ICAR-NIHSAD 开发。疫苗 针对的是被称为 Clade 2.3.2.1a 的 H5N1 病毒株,该病毒株与重大家禽损失和经济影响有关。该事件是根据农业和农民福利部的官方声明确认的。
规模、影响与风险
农业和农民福利部表示,一场重大禽流感暴发可能使印度20%以上的家禽种群面临风险,并使该国GDP增长减少高达1.5%。自2006年以来,该国已报告了500多起涉及H5N1和H5N8毒株的暴发组合病毒。H5N1 进化枝 2.3.2.1a 菌株也在其他动物物种和三例人类病例中被发现,引起了公众健康担忧。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 正在由 ICAR-NIHSAD 开发,预计于 2026 年 11 月 20 日推出。农业和农民福利部已概述了其在高风险农业地区的分发计划。疫苗 旨在遏制鸡群感染并减轻公共卫生风险。来源评估是官方的,可信度很高。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India to get first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens on November 20

News Brief
India is set to introduce its first indigenous vaccine against the H5N1 bird flu virus for chickens on November 20, 2026, developed by the ICAR-National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (ICAR-NIHSAD). The vaccine targets the H5N1 Clade 2.3.2.1a strain, which has been responsible for most bird flu outbreaks in the country and has also been detected in other animal species and three human cases. The Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare highlighted that a major bird flu outbreak could put more than 20% of India's poultry population at risk and reduce GDP growth by up to 1.5%. Since 2006, over 500 combined outbreaks involving H5N1 and H5N8 strains have been reported, causing significant economic losses. The vaccine aims to curb infections among chickens, safeguard vulnerable livestock populations, and prevent transmission to other animals and humans. The event is confirmed based on official statements and is part of India's efforts to address the growing threat of H5N1 bird flu.

Find out how authorities plan to roll out distribution across high-risk farming regions. +Follow on GoogleAdd as preferred source India is set to introduce its first indigenous vaccine against the H5N1 bird flu virus for chickens. Union Agriculture and Farmers Welfare Minister Shivraj Singh Chouhan will dedicate the vaccine to the nation on November 20, at ICAR-National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (ICAR-NIHSAD) in Bhopal. Developed by ICAR-NIHSAD, the inactivated vaccine targets the H5N1 virus strain known as Clade 2.3.2.1a. According to the Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare, it is the “first of its kind in India against the H5N1 HPAI virus of Clade 2.3.2.1a.” Why is the H5N1 vaccine important? Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI), also called bird flu, is a severe viral infection that affects poultry. Outbreaks can cause mortality rates of nearly 100% among infected birds, resulting in heavy losses for poultry farmers due to bird culling and trade restrictions. India first detected the disease in 2006. Since then, more than 500 combined outbreaks involving H5N1 and H5N8 strains have been reported in the country. The ministry said a major bird flu outbreak could put more than 20% of India's poultry population at risk and reduce the country's GDP growth by up to 1.5%. The 2006 outbreak alone caused estimated direct and indirect losses of Rs 2,200–3,000 crore. H5N1 strain poses wider concerns According to the ministry, the H5N1 Clade 2.3.2.1a strain has been responsible for most bird flu outbreaks in chickens. In recent years, infections involving this strain have also been detected in other animal species, including mammals, as well as in three human cases. The disease is also a public health concern. The ministry cited World Health Organisation data showing that 1,003 human cases of H5N1 infection, including 479 deaths, had been reported across 25 countries, including India, since 2003. How the indigenous vaccine will help The ministry said no specific commercial vaccine was available against the H5N1 Clade 2.3.2.1a strain. The indigenous vaccine developed by ICAR-NIHSAD is intended to provide protection against strains of the virus circulating in India. "This makes it important to control the spread of this virus to reduce financial losses for poultry farmers and prevent its transmission from chickens to other animals and humans," the ministry said. Shivraj Singh Chouhan 今天将印度首个针对家禽的本土 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗 奉献给全国

Shivraj Singh Chouhan to dedicate India’s first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for poultry to nation today
新闻简报On Friday, November 20, 2026, Union Minister Shivraj Singh Chouhan will dedicate India’s first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for poultry to the nation. The vaccine, developed by the Bhopal-based ICAR-NIHSAD, aims to protect poultry from the circulating H5N1 Clade 2.3.2.1a strain, reducing economic losses and limiting virus transmission to other animals and humans. Since its first reported case in India in 2006, the country has faced over 500 outbreaks involving H5N1 and H5N8 subtypes. These outbreaks have led to significant financial impacts, including estimated losses of Rs 2,200 crore to Rs 3,000 crore during the 2006 outbreak and losses equivalent to 0.5 per cent of Manipur’s GDP in a separate incident. The vaccine is a critical step in strengthening disease control in the poultry sector, as it addresses the lack of specific commercial protection against the H5N1 strain.

时间
据报道,该活动将于 2026 年 11 月 20 日星期五(N9)举行。
地点与对象
该活动与印度家禽业相关,疫苗 由位于博帕尔的 ICAR-NIHSAD (N10) 开发。
事件
联邦部长 Shivraj Singh Chouhan 将把印度首个针对家禽的本土 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗 献给国家 (N9)。
规模、影响与风险
自2006年以来,印度已经历了超过500起涉及H5N1和H5N8亚型(N14)的暴发。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 正在该研究所亮相,标志着向疾病控制 (N11) 迈出了一步。

Shivraj Singh Chouhan to dedicate India’s first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for poultry to nation today

News Brief
On Friday, November 20, 2026, Union Minister Shivraj Singh Chouhan will dedicate India’s first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for poultry to the nation. The vaccine, developed by the Bhopal-based ICAR-NIHSAD, aims to protect poultry from the circulating H5N1 Clade 2.3.2.1a strain, reducing economic losses and limiting virus transmission to other animals and humans. Since its first reported case in India in 2006, the country has faced over 500 outbreaks involving H5N1 and H5N8 subtypes. These outbreaks have led to significant financial impacts, including estimated losses of Rs 2,200 crore to Rs 3,000 crore during the 2006 outbreak and losses equivalent to 0.5 per cent of Manipur’s GDP in a separate incident. The vaccine is a critical step in strengthening disease control in the poultry sector, as it addresses the lack of specific commercial protection against the H5N1 strain.

Mangalorean.com Home Mangalorean News Local News Gulf News Kannada News Recipes Fit & Fun Sports Health Entertainment Other News Mangalorean Star Obituary Congratulations Sponsored Classifieds Best Hospitals & Institutions Best Schools & Colleges Your Thoughts Old Mangalorean Regional National Business General My Opinion Literature Advertise With Us World Home Agency News Shivraj Singh Chouhan to dedicate India’s first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine... Agency News National Shivraj Singh Chouhan to dedicate India’s first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for poultry to nation today By IANS - November 20, 2026 Spread the love Tweet Shivraj Singh Chouhan to dedicate India’s first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for poultry to nation today New Delhi: Union Minister of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare and Rural Development, Shivraj Singh Chouhan, will dedicate India’s first “indigenous inactivated H5N1 Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI) virus vaccine” for poultry to the nation on Friday. Bhopal-based ICAR-National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (ICAR-NIHSAD) has developed a vaccine against the deadly animal disease ‘Bird Flu’, also known as Avian Influenza, officials said. The vaccine will be unveiled at the institute and marks a major step toward strengthening disease control in the country’s poultry sector. Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI), widely known as bird flu, poses a grave risk to chickens and other avian species, with outbreaks often resulting in total flock mortality rates. In addition to necessitating mass culling and trade bans, bird flu disrupts international markets, drives up poultry product prices, and inflicts heavy financial losses on farmers. Since the first reported case in India in 2006, the country has experienced over 500 outbreaks involving the H5N1 and H5N8 subtypes. Official estimates indicate that a major outbreak could reduce India’s GDP growth by 1.5 per cent and put more than 20 per cent of the nation’s poultry population at risk. Past outbreaks have already demonstrated this financial vulnerability: the 2006 outbreak resulted in estimated direct and indirect losses of Rs 2,200 crore to Rs 3,000 crore, while a separate outbreak in Manipur caused losses equivalent to 0.5 per cent of the state’s GDP. In addition to agricultural losses, H5N1 carries serious public health implications. According to the World Health Organization (WHO), 1,003 human cases of H5N1 avian influenza — resulting in 479 deaths — have been reported across 25 countries, including India, since 2003. Virological studies show that the “H5N1 Clade 2.3.2.1a strain” has been responsible for most outbreaks in chickens, with recent infections also detected in mammals alongside three reported human cases. Until now, no specific commercial vaccine was available to protect poultry against the circulating Clade 2.3.2.1a strain. ICAR-NIHSAD’s indigenous vaccine fills this critical void by helping to protect poultry from the circulating strain, reducing economic losses for farmers, and limiting the virus’s transmission from chickens to other animals and humans. 印度将推出本土 H5N1 家禽 疫苗

India to unveil indigenous H5N1 poultry vaccine
新闻简报印度将于 2026 年 11 月 20 日星期五在博帕尔的 ICAR-NIHSAD 推出首个针对家禽的本土灭活 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗。该研究所开发的疫苗旨在保护家禽免受流行的H5N1毒株的侵害,减少农民的经济损失,并限制该病毒的传播对其他动物和人类的影响。自 2006 年报告首例病例以来,H5N1 已在印度造成 500 多只 暴发,对超过 20% 的家禽种群构成潜在风险,估计 GDP 损失达 1.5%。这种病毒被称为 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI),可导致鸡群总数 死亡率、大规模扑杀和贸易禁令,扰乱国际市场并提高家禽产品价格。疫苗 标志着疾病控制方面迈出的重要一步,也是政府应对 H5N1 威胁的努力的一部分。
查看新闻五要素
时间
据 N2 和 N3 报道,疫苗 将于 2026 年 11 月 20 日星期五亮相。
地点与对象
疫苗 由印度博帕尔的 ICAR-NIHSAD 开发,旨在用于该国的家禽行业,如 N2、N4 和 N18 中所述。
事件
印度将推出首个针对家禽的本土灭活 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗,该产品由 ICAR-NIHSAD 开发,已在 N2、N3 和 N17 中得到证实。
规模、影响与风险
自 2006 年以来,H5N1 已在印度造成 500 多起 暴发,如 N9 和 N10 中所述,给家禽种群带来潜在的经济损失和风险。正如 N8 中提到的,该病毒可能导致鸡群总数 死亡率、大规模扑杀和贸易禁令。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 的推出是为了应对 H5N1 的威胁,官员们表示它将保护家禽并减少经济损失,如 N16 和 N19 中详述。消息来源是一家信誉良好的新闻出版商,该信息已得到政府公告的证实。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India to unveil indigenous H5N1 poultry vaccine

News Brief
India is set to unveil its first indigenous inactivated H5N1 bird flu vaccine for poultry on Friday, November 20, 2026, at ICAR-NIHSAD in Bhopal. The vaccine, developed by the institute, aims to protect poultry against the circulating H5N1 strain, reduce economic losses for farmers, and limit the virus’s transmission to other animals and humans. Since its first reported case in 2006, H5N1 has caused over 500 outbreaks in India, with potential risks to more than 20% of the poultry population and estimated GDP losses of 1.5%. The virus, known as Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI), can lead to total flock mortality, mass culling, and trade bans, disrupting international markets and raising poultry product prices. The vaccine marks a significant step in disease control and is part of the government’s efforts to address the threat posed by H5N1.

By Telangana Today Desk Published Date - 20 November 2026, 07:22 AM Union Minister for Agriculture and Farmers Welfare and Rural Development Shivraj Singh Chouhan will dedicate India’s first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for poultry to the nation at ICAR-NIHSAD, Bhopal, today. ICAR-NIHSAD developed indigenous vaccine New Delhi: Union Minister for Agriculture and Farmers Welfare and Rural Development Shivraj Singh Chouhan will dedicate India’s first “indigenous inactivated H5N1 Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI) virus vaccine” for poultry to the nation on Friday. The Bhopal-based Indian Council of Agricultural Research-National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (ICAR-NIHSAD) developed the vaccine against bird flu, also known as avian influenza, officials said. The vaccine will be unveiled at the institute and marks a major step towards strengthening disease control in the country’s poultry sector, officials said. Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI), widely known as bird flu, poses a risk to chickens and other avian species, with outbreaks often resulting in total flock mortality. Bird flu also necessitates mass culling and trade bans, disrupts international markets, raises poultry product prices and causes financial losses to farmers. Since the first reported case in India in 2006, the country has recorded over 500 outbreaks involving the H5N1 and H5N8 subtypes. Official estimates indicate that a major outbreak could reduce India’s GDP growth by 1.5 per cent and put more than 20 per cent of the nation’s poultry population at risk. The 2006 outbreak resulted in estimated direct and indirect losses of Rs 2,200 crore to Rs 3,000 crore, while a separate outbreak in Manipur caused losses equivalent to 0.5 per cent of the State’s GDP. H5N1 also has public health implications. According to the World Health Organization (WHO), 1,003 human cases of H5N1 avian influenza, resulting in 479 deaths, have been reported across 25 countries, including India, since 2003. Virological studies show that the “H5N1 Clade 2.3.2.1a strain” has been responsible for most outbreaks in chickens, with recent infections also detected in mammals alongside three reported human cases. Until now, no specific commercial vaccine was available to protect poultry against the circulating Clade 2.3.2.1a strain. ICAR-NIHSAD’s indigenous vaccine will help protect poultry from the circulating strain, reduce economic losses for farmers and limit the virus’s transmission from chickens to other animals and humans, officials said. In short India’s first indigenous inactivated H5N1 Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza vaccine for poultry will be dedicated to the nation on Friday. The Indian Council of Agricultural Research-National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases developed the vaccine in Bhopal. Officials said the vaccine will protect poultry against the circulating strain and reduce losses to farmers. India will unveil its first indigenous inactivated H5N1 bird flu vaccine for poultry, developed by ICAR-NIHSAD, as confirmed in N2, N3, and N17.
Scale, impact and risk
H5N1 has caused over 500 outbreaks in India since 2006, with potential economic losses and risks to poultry populations, as stated in N9 and N10. The virus can lead to total flock mortality, mass culling, and trade bans, as mentioned in N8.
Response, status and uncertainty
The vaccine is being unveiled as a response to the threat of H5N1, with officials stating it will protect poultry and reduce economic losses, as detailed in N16 and N19. The source is a reputable news publisher, and the information is confirmed by the government's announcement.

India unveils 1st homegrown H5N1 vaccine to combat bird flu amid rising outbreaks - Deccan Herald

India unveils 1st homegrown H5N1 vaccine to combat bird flu amid rising outbreaks - Deccan Herald
新闻简报India has launched its first indigenously developed vaccine against the H5N1 bird flu virus, a critical step in combating outbreaks linked to the Clade 2.3.2.1a strain. The vaccine, developed by the Indian Council of Agricultural Research (ICAR) and the National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (NIHSAD), targets the H5N1 virus responsible for nearly 100 per cent mortality in infected birds, causing major financial losses for poultry farmers. The strain has also been detected in mammals and three human cases, raising public health concerns. Currently, there is no specific vaccine available in India for this strain, making the new development essential for controlling the spread and protecting both animal and human populations. The vaccine is expected to address the circulating H5N1 strains in the country, enhancing preparedness against the highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) outbreaks.

印度在新鲜禽流感 暴发 中开发自己的 H5N1 疫苗 - 联邦

India develops its own H5N1 vaccine amid fresh bird flu outbreaks - The Federal
新闻简报印度开发了第一个针对 H5N1 禽流感 的本土 疫苗,针对分支 2.3.2.1a 菌株,该菌株是导致鸡中大多数禽流感 暴发 的原因。疫苗 由 ICAR-NIHSAD 开发,可提供长达六个月的保护,适用于蛋鸡和种鸡。这一进展正值泰米尔纳德邦和安得拉邦发生新鲜禽流感 暴发 之际,截至 11 月 13 日,该地区已有 35,647 只禽类死亡,约 40,000 只鸡被扑杀。当局实施了行动限制,并宣布感染区和 监测 区。H5N1 毒株也在哺乳动物和 3 例人类病例中被发现,世界卫生组织报告自 2003 年以来全球有 1,003 例人类病例和 479 例死亡。疫苗 预计将加强印度对 暴发 的准备,以及 监测、测试、生物安全和扑杀等现有措施。联邦农业部长 Shivraj Singh Chouhan 将于 11 月 20 日向全国奉献 疫苗。
查看新闻五要素
时间
疫苗 的开发于 2026 年 11 月 18 日宣布,泰米尔纳德邦的 H5N1 暴发 于 11 月得到确认,截至 11 月 13 日报告有 35,647 只鸟类死亡。
地点与对象
泰米尔纳德邦和安得拉邦,特别是古达洛尔地区和阿纳纳塔布尔地区,报告了 暴发 疫情,影响了家禽并导致扑杀和行动限制。
事件
印度已开发出首个针对 H5N1 禽流感 的本土 疫苗,其目标是分支 2.3.2.1a 的 H5N1 病毒,该病毒是导致鸡中大多数禽流感 暴发 的原因。这一发展是在新鲜禽流感 暴发 和扑杀措施期间发生的。
规模、影响与风险
截至11月13日,受影响区域已有35,647只鸟类死亡,约40,000只鸡被扑杀。H5N1 clade 2.3.2.1a毒株已在不同动物物种中检测到,并在三例人类病例中发现。世界卫生组织自2003年以来报告了1,003例人类H5N1感染病例,包括479例死亡,涉及25个国家。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 预计将为印度控制 暴发 的努力添加另一个工具,与 监测 一起,用于测试、生物安全和扑杀。联邦农业部长将于 11 月 20 日将 疫苗 献给全国。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India develops its own H5N1 vaccine amid fresh bird flu outbreaks - The Federal

News Brief
India has developed its first indigenous vaccine against H5N1 avian influenza, targeting the clade 2.3.2.1a strain, which has been responsible for most bird flu outbreaks in chickens. The vaccine, developed by ICAR-NIHSAD, provides protection for up to six months and is suitable for layer and breeder chickens. This development comes amid fresh bird flu outbreaks in Tamil Nadu and Andhra Pradesh, where 35,647 birds had died as of November 13, and around 40,000 chickens were culled. Authorities have imposed movement restrictions and declared infected and surveillance zones. The H5N1 strain has also been detected in mammals and three human cases, with the World Health Organization reporting 1,003 human cases and 479 deaths globally since 2003. The vaccine is expected to enhance India’s preparedness for outbreaks, alongside existing measures such as surveillance, testing, biosecurity, and culling. The Union Agriculture Minister Shivraj Singh Chouhan will dedicate the vaccine to the nation on November 20.
Original source text (English)

人参皂苷 Rg1 佐剂增强鸡对 H9N2 禽流感 的免疫保护 - Bioengineer.org

Ginsenoside Rg1 Adjuvant Boosts Immune Protection Against H9N2 Avian Influenza in Chickens - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 11 月 14 日发表的一项研究调查了使用人参皂苷 Rg1 作为佐剂来增强鸡对 H9N2 禽流感 的免疫保护。该研究在中国进行,涉及 150 只一日龄 Arbor Acres 肉鸡,分为三组:未添加佐剂的 疫苗、添加 Rg1 的 疫苗 和对照组。结果表明,与单独使用 疫苗接种 相比,添加人参皂苷 Rg1 显着提高了特异性免疫球蛋白水平和血凝抑制滴度。在病毒攻击后,Rg1 组的鸟类表现出病毒脱落减少和关键组织中病毒载量降低。该研究还强调了 H9N2 抗原变异和基因重组的风险,这可能导致人畜共患威胁。虽然研究结果表明人参皂苷 Rg1 可以提高 疫苗 的功效,但还需要进一步的现场验证来确认其在现实家禽操作中的有效性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究发表于 2026 年 11 月 14 日。该实验是在第 0、14 和 28 天接种疫苗的鸟类身上进行的,并对结果进行了分析直至免疫后第 42 天。
地点与对象
该研究在中国进行,使用来自经过认证的商业孵化场的 Arbor Acres 肉鸡。种群中包括 150 只一日大的未免疫雏鸡,分为三组,每组 50 只。
事件
研究人员证明,用人参皂苷 Rg1 补充灭活的 H9N2 禽流感 疫苗 可以显着增强 免疫应答 和幼鸡的保护功效,病毒攻击后免疫球蛋白水平更高,病毒脱落减少就证明了这一点。
规模、影响与风险
该研究强调了人参皂苷 Rg1 作为天然佐剂提高 疫苗 对抗 H9N2 禽流感 有效性的潜力。它还指出了 H9N2 病毒株中抗原变异和基因重排的持续风险,这可能导致种间 传播 和人畜共患对人类健康的威胁。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是根据批准的机构动物护理方案进行的,采用严格的方法,包括对照试验、免疫学测定和组织病理学评估。这些发现基于实验数据,为改进家禽免疫计划提供了一个有希望的方向,但还需要进一步的现场验证。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Ginsenoside Rg1 Adjuvant Boosts Immune Protection Against H9N2 Avian Influenza in Chickens - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published on November 14, 2026, by Bioengineer.org investigated the use of ginsenoside Rg1 as an adjuvant to enhance immune protection against H9N2 avian influenza in chickens. The research, conducted in China, involved 150 one-day-old Arbor Acres broiler chicks divided into three groups: unadjuvanted vaccine, Rg1-supplemented vaccine, and control. Results showed that the addition of ginsenoside Rg1 significantly increased specific immunoglobulin levels and hemagglutination inhibition titers compared to vaccination alone. Following a viral challenge, birds in the Rg1 group exhibited reduced viral shedding and lower viral loads in critical tissues. The study also emphasized the risks of H9N2's antigenic variation and genetic reassortment, which can lead to zoonotic threats. While the findings suggest ginsenoside Rg1 could improve vaccine efficacy, further field validation is required to confirm its effectiveness in real-world poultry operations.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, November 20, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Ginsenoside Rg1 Adjuvant Boosts Immune Protection Against H9N2 Avian Influenza in Chickens by November 14, 2026 in Biology, Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Ginsenoside Rg1 Adjuvant Boosts Immune Protection Against H9N2 Avian Influenza in Chickens Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Researchers investigating novel poultry immunization strategies have demonstrated that supplementing an inactivated H9N2 avian influenza vaccine with ginsenoside Rg1 substantially enhances immune responses and protective efficacy in young chickens. In a controlled trial conducted using one-day-old non-immunized Arbor Acres broiler chicks, administering ginsenoside Rg1 alongside the standard inactivated H9N2 vaccine generated significantly higher levels of specific immunoglobulins and hemagglutination inhibition titers compared to vaccination alone. Following an experimental intranasal viral challenge with live avian influenza, birds that received the adjuvant regimen exhibited marked reductions in oropharyngeal and cloacal viral shedding, alongside lower viral loads within critical respiratory and renal tissues. These findings indicate that ginsenoside Rg1 holds strong potential as an effective natural adjuvant for avian influenza vaccination programs. The global poultry sector faces persistent operational and economic setbacks driven by the continuous circulation of the H9N2 subtype, which represents the most widespread form of low pathogenic avian influenza found in domestic bird populations. More critically, ongoing genomic monitoring reveals that circulating H9N2 lineages continuously undergo antigenic variation and genetic reassortment within agricultural reservoirs. Through these evolutionary shifts, H9N2 strains frequently donate essential internal genetic segments to emerging highly pathogenic avian influenza variants, including lethal subtypes such as H5N1 and H7N9. This genetic exchange significantly amplifies the risk of interspecies transmission and poses ongoing zoonotic threats to human public health. To counteract widespread circulation, agricultural operations in numerous countries, including China, South Korea, and Egypt, routinely administer oil-adjuvanted inactivated vaccines to poultry flocks. Nevertheless, recurrent field outbreaks persist even among heavily vaccinated bird populations, exposing critical shortcomings in current immunization programs that largely stem from the limited antigenic breadth and suboptimal immunogenicity of existing formulations. Vaccine effectiveness depends fundamentally not only on target antigen selection but also on the pharmacological properties of the adjuvant used to stimulate the host immune system. While commercial animal vaccines commonly incorporate adjuvants such as Matrix-M, CpG1018, MF59, or traditional aluminum salts, these agents present notable practical limitations. Many conventional formulations fail to generate adequate cellular immune activation, carry potential risks of adverse reactions, or induce pronounced local tissue irritation at injection sites. These recognized biological drawbacks highlight an urgent need in veterinary medicine to identify safe, non-toxic adjuvants capable of enhancing cellular and humoral protection without compromising animal welfare. Discover more Check Symptoms Vaccine vaccine vaccines Find Vaccines In the search for safer alternatives, natural pharmacological compounds derived from traditional botanical medicine have attracted growing scientific attention due to their low intrinsic toxicity, high biocompatibility, and diverse immunomodulatory capabilities. Previous immunological studies have established that ginsenoside Rg1 possesses the capacity to stimulate balanced T helper type 1 and T helper type 2 immune responses in multiple animal species. Scientific literature has documented its adjuvant efficacy in experimental vaccination models against several major animal and human viral pathogens, including hepatitis B virus, infectious bursal disease virus, influenza, and porcine reproductive and respiratory syndrome virus. However, despite these encouraging demonstrations in other disease contexts, the functional capacity of ginsenoside Rg1 to bolster protective immunity against avian H9N2 influenza in commercial poultry had remained entirely unexplored prior to this comprehensive study. To rigorously evaluate the adjuvant capabilities of the compound, the research team procured high-grade ginsenoside Rg1 possessing a verified chemical purity exceeding 98 percent from a specialized biochemical supplier in Chengdu, China. Recognizing that bacterial endotoxin contamination can confound immunological evaluations by introducing uncontrolled inflammatory artifacts, the investigators processed the saponin solution through a dedicated gel endotoxin-removal column, successfully verifying that residual endotoxin concentrations remained strictly below 0.5 endotoxin units per milliliter. The viral component utilized throughout the immunization and challenge trials was the low pathogenic avian influenza serotype strain designated A/Chicken/Jilin/DH109/2012, which was propagated and adapted in nine-day-old specific-pathogen-free embryonated chicken eggs. Commercial inactivated H9N2 vaccines were acquired from Harbin Pharmaceutical Group Biological Vaccine Company. The experimental cohort comprised 150 one-day-old non-immunized Arbor Acres chicks obtained from a certified commercial hatchery, which were randomly divided into three identical treatment groups of 50 chicks each to establish rigorous comparative baselines under approved institutional animal care protocols. Discover more Book Acupuncture Identify Plants Try Herbs The experimental protocol evaluated three distinct cohorts: an unadjuvanted vaccine group, an Rg1-supplemented vaccine group, and an unvaccinated control group receiving sterile phosphate-buffered saline. Birds in both vaccinated groups were administered 200 microliters of the inactivated H9N2 vaccine by intramuscular injection on three occasions spaced exactly two weeks apart, on days 0, 14, and 28. Concurrently with the initial immunization, the adjuvant group received ginsenoside Rg1 dissolved in drinking water at a calculated dosage of 1 milligram per kilogram of body weight daily for seven consecutive days. Within each 50-bird group, specific biological replicates were systematically assigned to pre-planned investigative endpoints: ten live chickens were monitored longitudinally for blood serum antibodies and mucosal swabs; 18 were sampled across scheduled intervals for splenic lymphocyte proliferation assays; five were evaluated for immune organ weights; five underwent hepatic and renal histopathological examination; nine were assessed for organ viral loads; and 13 were observed continuously for clinical health signs, body weight development, and survival. Humoral immune responses were tracked by collecting serum samples on days 14, 28, and 42 following the initial vaccination to measure specific immunoglobulin G concentrations and hemagglutination inhibition titers. Enzymatic analysis revealed that while the standard vaccine induced detectable antibodies above control levels, the addition of ginsenoside Rg1 produced a marked enhancement in antibody production. By days 28 and 42, specific serum immunoglobulin G levels were significantly elevated in the Rg1 combination group relative to birds receiving the unadjuvanted vaccine (p < 0.05). Furthermore, hemagglutination inhibition titers in the adjuvant group displayed a sustained, statistically significant rise throughout the 42-day observation window. Because a hemagglutination inhibition titer of at least 4 log2 is widely recognized as the definitive benchmark required for neutralizing viral particles, preventing hemagglutinin-receptor binding, and blocking viral transmission, the robust titers achieved in the Rg1 cohort underscore the compound’s capacity to amplify protective neutralizing antibody synthesis. Beyond antibody production, the researchers assessed cell-mediated immunity, which plays a pivotal role in clearing intracellular viral infections and establishing durable immunological memory. Splenic lymphocytes harvested on days 14, 28, and 42 post-immunization were tested using Cell Counting Kit-8 colorimetric assays to evaluate proliferation dynamics. At days 28 and 42, lymphocytes isolated from chicks receiving ginsenoside Rg1 exhibited significantly higher proliferative responses compared to the vaccine-only group (p < 0.05). Complementary multi-parameter flow cytometry revealed that the proportions of both CD3+CD4+ helper T cells and CD3+CD8+ cytotoxic T cells were markedly expanded in the Rg1 group relative to controls. In parallel, enzyme-linked immunosorbent assays showed that the adjuvant regimen significantly enhanced serum concentrations of key regulatory cytokines, including interferon-gamma, tumor necrosis factor-alpha, interleukin-4, and interleukin-6. This coordinated upregulation demonstrates that ginsenoside Rg1 simultaneously potentiates cytotoxic CD8+ cellular effector pathways and promotes Th2-associated cytokine production responsible for directing optimal B-cell maturation. The investigation also confirmed that ginsenoside Rg1 supplementation supported healthy lymphoid organ development without inducing systemic organ toxicity. On day 28 post-inoculation, chickens in the adjuvant cohort exhibited significantly increased weight indices for the spleen, thymus, and bursa of Fabricius compared to those receiving vaccine alone. Biochemical screening of serum alkaline phosphatase and lactate dehydrogenase revealed no abnormal deviations, while histopathological examination of liver and kidney sections demonstrated normal tissue architecture without pathological lesions across all vaccinated groups. Fourteen days following the final immunization, birds were challenged intranasally with 100 microliters containing 10^7 median egg infectious doses of the live H9N2 strain. Swab sampling assessed by quantitative real-time PCR revealed that while unvaccinated controls exhibited 90 percent shedding rates at day 14 and vaccine-only birds shed virus through day 10, the Rg1 group completely cleared oropharyngeal and cloacal shedding by day 10. Moreover, tissue viral load assessments confirmed that live virus was undetectable in the lungs and kidneys of Rg1-treated chickens by day 10 post-challenge. In evaluating the overall outcomes, the authors noted that combining oral ginsenoside Rg1 with an inactivated H9N2 vaccine successfully stimulated both branches of the avian adaptive immune system, resulting in accelerated viral clearance and superior tissue protection. The botanical adjuvant enhanced the production of protective neutralizing antibodies, expanded functional T-cell subsets, and bolstered key cytokine networks while maintaining an excellent biosafety profile. Nonetheless, the researchers explicitly acknowledged two important methodological limitations in their experimental design. First, the toxicological screening was restricted to targeted liver and kidney biochemistry and histopathology, rather than an exhaustive safety assessment adhering strictly to international World Health Organization or European Medicines Agency regulatory standards. Second, the study did not incorporate an isolated control cohort receiving ginsenoside Rg1 monotherapy without vaccination, precluding direct observation of the adjuvant’s baseline physiological effects in unimmunized animals. Subject of Research: Ginsenoside Rg1 as an adjuvant for inactivated H9N2 avian influenza vaccination in chickens Article Title: Ginsenoside Rg1 as an adjuvant enhances immunity in chickens immunized with the H9N2 avian influenza inactivated vaccine Article References: Zhang, P., Wang, H., Li, M., Lyu, X., Gao, Z., Wang, W., Li, X., & Zhang, L. (2026). Ginsenoside Rg1 as an adjuvant enhances immunity in chickens immunized with the H9N2 avian influenza inactivated vaccine. View five news elements
Time
The study was published on November 14, 2026. The experimental trial was conducted with birds vaccinated on days 0, 14, and 28, and the results were analyzed up to day 42 post-immunization.
Location and population
The study was conducted in China, using Arbor Acres broiler chicks from a certified commercial hatchery. The population included 150 one-day-old non-immunized chicks, divided into three groups of 50 each.
Event
Researchers demonstrated that supplementing an inactivated H9N2 avian influenza vaccine with ginsenoside Rg1 significantly enhances immune responses and protective efficacy in young chickens, as evidenced by higher immunoglobulin levels and reduced viral shedding following a viral challenge.
Scale, impact and risk
The study highlights the potential of ginsenoside Rg1 as a natural adjuvant to improve vaccine effectiveness against H9N2 avian influenza. It also notes the ongoing risks of antigenic variation and genetic reassortment in H9N2 strains, which can lead to interspecies transmission and zoonotic threats to human health.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was conducted under approved institutional animal care protocols, with rigorous methods including controlled trials, immunological assays, and histopathological evaluations. The findings are based on experimental data and suggest a promising direction for improving poultry immunization programs, though further field validation is needed.

印度开发出首个本土鸡用 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗 - 新印度快报

India develops first home-grown H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens - The New Indian Express
新闻简报印度研发出了首个本土鸡用H5N1禽流感病毒疫苗,标志着在对抗高致病性禽流感(HPAI)方面取得了重大突破。疫苗 基于本土开发的技术,针对 H5N1(分支 2.3.2.1a)毒株,该毒株在 2006 年至 2021 年间导致 24 个州的 284 例 暴发 感染。H5N1 毒株还蔓延至哺乳动物,并导致最近的三例人类病例。HPAI 死亡率 可导致受感染禽类中近 100% 死亡率,由于大规模扑杀和贸易限制,给家禽养殖户造成重大经济损失。ICAR-NIHSAD 主任 Aniket Sanyal 表示,疫苗 的开发是保障家禽健康和促进兽医生物制品自力更生的一个重要里程碑。然而,目前还没有专门针对Clade 2.3.2.1a的H5N1病毒的疫苗,疫苗对预防未来暴发的影响还有待观察。
查看新闻五要素
时间
印度 印度开发出首个本土 H5N1 禽流感病毒 疫苗。由于大规模扑杀鸟类和贸易限制,给家禽养殖户带来了巨大的经济损失。
地点与对象
印度 24 个邦的家禽养殖户和鸡。
事件
印度开发出了首个本土鸡用 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗,该病毒基于本土开发的技术,针对 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 病毒的 H5N1(分支 2.3.2.1a)毒株。
规模、影响与风险
2006年至2021年间,印度经历了341起暴发禽流感病例,其中284起病例归因于H5N1,57起病例归因于H5N8。H5N1 病毒株已蔓延至哺乳动物,并导致最近三起人类病例。HPAI 死亡率 可导致受感染禽类中近 100% 死亡率,从而给家禽养殖户造成重大经济损失,并可能对全球贸易造成干扰。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 即将推出,由位于博帕尔的印度农业研究委员会国家高安全动物疾病研究所 (ICAR-NIHSAD) 的科学家开发。该开发被认为是对抗 HPAI 的一个重要里程碑,但目前还没有专门针对 Clade 2.3.2.1a 的 H5N1 病毒的 疫苗。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

India develops first home-grown H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens - The New Indian Express

News Brief
India has developed its first home-grown H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens, marking a significant breakthrough in combating Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI). The vaccine, based on indigenously developed technology, targets the H5N1 (Clade 2.3.2.1a) strain, which has been responsible for 284 outbreaks across 24 states between 2006 and 2021. The H5N1 strain has also spilled over into mammals and caused three recent human cases. HPAI outbreaks can lead to nearly 100% mortality among infected birds, causing substantial financial losses for poultry farmers due to large-scale culling and trade restrictions. The development of this vaccine is a major milestone in safeguarding poultry health and advancing self-reliance in veterinary biologicals, as stated by Aniket Sanyal, Director of ICAR-NIHSAD. However, there is currently no specific vaccine against the H5N1 virus of Clade 2.3.2.1a, and the vaccine's impact on preventing future outbreaks remains to be seen.
Original source text (English)
India India develops first home-grown H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens This results in substantial financial losses for poultry farmers due to large-scale culling of birds and trade restrictions. NEW DELHI: In a major breakthrough after years of research and trials, India is set to launch its first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine, raising hopes within the poultry industry of preventing recurring bird flu outbreaks. The vaccine, based on indigenously developed technology, is an Inactivated H5N1 (Clade 2.3.2.1a) formulation that protects against the Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI) virus strain circulating in the country. Of late, this dangerous strain has also spilled over in various animal species, including mammals, along with three recent human cases. Between 2006 and 2021, India experienced 341 outbreaks of bird flu across 24 states. Of these, 284 cases were attributed to the H5N1 virus and 57 to H5N8. While there is a vaccine available for lower-risk pathogens, there is currently no specific vaccine against the H5N1 virus of Clade 2.3.2.1a. The vaccine was developed by scientists at the Indian Council for Agricultural Research-National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (ICAR-NIHSAD) in Bhopal. “This marks a major milestone in safeguarding poultry health, strengthening farm resilience, and advancing Atmanirbharta (self-reliance) in veterinary biologicals,” Aniket Sanyal, Director of ICAR-NIHSAD, told this daily. The development of this vaccine represents a significant achievement for Indian science in combating Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI), commonly known as bird flu. HPAI is a severe disease, and outbreaks can lead to nearly 100% mortality among infected birds. This results in substantial financial losses for poultry farmers due to large-scale culling of birds and trade restrictions. The impact is not limited to poultry farms; it can also disrupt global trade, inflate prices of poultry products, and threaten the livelihoods of farmers. PAI has a near 100% mortality among infected birds. A significant outbreak of bird flu could reduce India’s GDP growth by up to 1.5%, with more than 20% of the country’s poultry population at risk if the disease spreads unchecked. The 2006 bird flu outbreak caused estimated direct and indirect losses between Rs 2,200 crore and Rs 3,000 crore
View five news elements
Time
India India develops first home-grown H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens This results in substantial financial losses for poultry farmers due to large-scale culling of birds and trade restrictions.
Location and population
India, poultry farmers, and chickens across 24 states.
Event
India has developed its first home-grown H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens, which is based on indigenously developed technology and targets the H5N1 (Clade 2.3.2.1a) strain of Highly Pathogenic Avian Influenza (HPAI) virus.
Scale, impact and risk
Between 2006 and 2021, India experienced 341 outbreaks of bird flu, with 284 cases attributed to H5N1 and 57 to H5N8. The H5N1 strain has spilled over into mammals and caused three recent human cases. HPAI outbreaks can lead to nearly 100% mortality among infected birds, resulting in substantial financial losses for poultry farmers and potential disruptions to global trade.
Response, status and uncertainty
The vaccine is set to be launched, and it is developed by scientists at the Indian Council for Agricultural Research-National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (ICAR-NIHSAD) in Bhopal. The development is considered a major milestone in combating HPAI, but there is currently no specific vaccine against the H5N1 virus of Clade 2.3.2.1a.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

印度将于 11 月 20 日推出首个针对鸡的本土 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗 - Medical Dialogues

India Set to Launch First Indigenous H5N1 Bird Flu Vaccine for Chickens on November 20 - Medical Dialogues
新闻简报India is preparing to launch its first indigenous H5N1 bird flu vaccine for chickens on November 20, 2026, as reported by Medical Dialogues on November 19. The vaccine, developed by scientists at the ICAR-National Institute of High Security Animal Diseases (NIHSAD) in Bhopal, targets the highly pathogenic avian influenza H5N1 virus, Clade 2.3.2.1a, which has been responsible for 284 outbreaks in the country between 2006 and 2021. Highly pathogenic avian influenza can lead to near-total mortality in poultry, causing significant economic losses, as seen in the 2006 outbreak which reportedly cost between Rs 2,200 crore and Rs 3,000 crore. The vaccine aims to enhance poultry disease prevention and reduce the impact of recurring outbreaks, which have led to large-scale culling and trade restrictions. The vaccine is intended for poultry, not human use, and is described as a milestone in India's self-reliance in veterinary vaccines.
查看新闻五要素
时间
据 2026 年 11 月 19 日发布的报道,印度准备于 2026 年 11 月 20 日推出首个本土 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗。
地点与对象
疫苗 适用于印度的家禽,2006 年至 2021 年间,印度在 24 个邦记录了 341 起禽流感 暴发,其中 284 起由 H5N1 引起,57 起由 H5N8 引起。
事件
印度即将推出首个针对鸡的本土 H5N1 禽流感 疫苗,由博帕尔 ICAR 国家高安全动物疾病研究所 (NIHSAD) 的科学家开发。
规模、影响与风险
疫苗预计将加强家禽疾病预防,减轻经常性暴发的经济负担,死亡率可能导致感染家禽死亡率接近100%,导致大规模扑杀、贸易限制和重大经济损失。
应对、状态与不确定性
疫苗 的发展被描述为加强家禽健康和印度兽医 疫苗 自力更生的一个重要里程碑。疫苗 适合家禽,不适用于人类。

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

又发生禽流感病例后实施限制

Restrictions in place after another bird flu case
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 19 日,英国林肯郡确认了一例新的禽流感病例,具体为 禽流感 H5N1。在东林赛 Coningsby 附近的商业家禽中发现了 暴发,促使环境、食品和农村事务部 (Defra) 宣布在周围设立 1.9 英里(3 公里)的保护区和 6.2 英里(10 公里)的 暴发 区域。该网站。作为应对措施的一部分,受影响地区的所有鸟类都将被人道扑杀。这是本月东约克郡和林肯郡发生的第四起 暴发 病例,布里格、波克灵顿和马基特雷森也报告了病例。作为回应,英国首席兽医官发布了一项全国命令,要求拥有超过 50 只禽类的家禽主人将其安置在室内。这些措施旨在遏制病毒传播并保护动物和人类健康。目前情况仍在积极调查中,并持续努力监控和管理 暴发。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 11 月 19 日,林肯郡确认了另一例禽流感病例。全国命令于上周出台,本月布里格、波克灵顿和马基特雷森地区报告了病例。
地点与对象
暴发 在林肯郡东林赛康宁斯比附近的商业家禽中被发现,影响了当地家禽数量。
事件
在东林赛康宁斯比附近的商业家禽中确认了 禽流感 H5N1 的 暴发,所有家禽均被人道扑杀。这是本月东约克郡和林肯郡报告的第四起 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
这是本月东约克郡和林肯郡报告的第四起 暴发 病例,布里格、波克灵顿和马基特雷森地区也报告了病例。该地点周围已宣布设立 1.9 英里(3 公里)保护区和 6.2 英里(10 公里)监测 区域。
应对、状态与不确定性
Defra 已宣布该地点周围为保护区和 监测 区,所有鸟类都将被人道扑杀。英国首席兽医官提出了一项国家命令,要求饲养超过 50 只家禽的家禽所有者确保将它们饲养在室内。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Restrictions in place after another bird flu case

News Brief
A new case of bird flu, specifically avian influenza H5N1, has been confirmed in Lincolnshire, UK, on 19 November 2026. The outbreak was found in commercial poultry near Coningsby in East Lindsey, prompting the Department for Environment, Food and Rural Affairs (Defra) to declare a 1.9 miles (3km) protection zone and a 6.2 miles (10km) surveillance zone around the site. All birds at the affected location will be humanely culled as part of the response. This marks the fourth outbreak in East Yorkshire and Lincolnshire this month, with cases also reported in Brigg, Pocklington, and Market Rasen. In response, the UK's Chief Veterinary Officer issued a national order requiring poultry owners with more than 50 birds to house them indoors. The measures aim to contain the spread of the virus and protect both animal and human health. The situation remains under active investigation, with ongoing efforts to monitor and manage the outbreak.
Original source text (English)
BBC Homepage Skip to content Accessibility Help Your account Home News Sport Earth Reel Worklife Travel Culture Future Music TV Weather Sounds More menu More menu Search BBC Home News Sport Earth Reel Worklife Travel Culture Future Music TV Weather Sounds Close menu BBC News Menu Home Climate World US & Canada UK Business Tech Science Entertainment & Arts Health More In Pictures BBC Verify Newsbeat England Regions Lincolnshire Restrictions in place after another bird flu case Image caption, Birds at the site near Coningsby will be humanely killed, Defra says ByStuart Harratt East Yorkshire and Lincolnshire Published 19 November 2026 Another case of bird flu has been confirmed in Lincolnshire. The Department for Environment, Food and Rural Affairs (Defra) said an outbreak of the avian influenza H5N1 was found in commercial poultry near Coningsby in East Lindsey. A 1.9 miles (3km) protection zone and 6.2 miles (10km) surveillance zone have been declared around the site and all birds will be humanely culled, Defra said. It is the fourth outbreak reported in East Yorkshire and Lincolnshire this month, with cases reported in the Brigg, Pocklington and Market Rasen areas. Last week, the UK's Chief Veterinary Officer introduced a national order directing all poultry owners with more than 50 birds to ensure they are housed indoors. Bird flu, also known as avian influenza, is an infectious disease affecting birds and can be spread through saliva, faeces or contaminated environments. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

俄勒冈州莫罗县奶牛场有人感染禽流感

Human contracts bird flu at Morrow County dairy farm in Oregon
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 19 日,俄勒冈州卫生局确认该州首例 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 人类病例,与莫罗县奶牛场的一头牛 暴发 有关。该患者出现轻微症状,接受了奥司他韦治疗,但没有住院。公共卫生官员强调,一般人群面临的风险“极低”,因为目前尚不清楚该病毒是否会在人与人之间传播。自 2022 年以来,已有 300 多人因接触患病动物而受到监测,其中 2024 年发现一例与家禽 暴发 有关的人类病例。俄勒冈州农业部和莫罗县公共卫生部门正在合作,确保受影响的农场工人得到及时检测和 Z​​XQTERMUQXZ。官员们还指出,季节性流感 疫苗 不能预防高致病性禽流感 (HPAI),但可能有助于降低人类和禽类病毒同时感染的风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
俄勒冈州卫生局于 11 月证实了这一病例。
地点与对象
俄勒冈州莫罗县奶牛场,自 2022 年以来已有 300 多人接触患病动物。
事件
确认了一起 高致病性禽流感 (HPAI) 人类病例,该病例与莫罗县奶牛场的一头牛 暴发 有关。
规模、影响与风险
发现一例人类病例,症状轻微,没有住院治疗。公众面临的风险被描述为“极低”。
应对、状态与不确定性
流行病学家正在与俄勒冈州农业部和莫罗县公共卫生部门合作,监测接触者。作为预防措施,向该患者的家人提供了奥司他韦。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Human contracts bird flu at Morrow County dairy farm in Oregon

News Brief
On November 19, 2026, the Oregon Health Authority confirmed the state's first human case of highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) linked to a cattle outbreak at a Morrow County dairy farm. The individual, who developed mild symptoms, was treated with oseltamivir but not hospitalized. Public health officials emphasized that the risk to the general population is "extremely low," as the virus is not known to spread from human to human. Over 300 people have been monitored for exposure to sick animals since 2022, with one human case identified in 2024 related to a poultry outbreak. The Oregon Department of Agriculture and Morrow County Public Health are collaborating to ensure affected farm workers receive prompt testing and treatment. Officials also noted that the seasonal flu vaccine does not protect against HPAI, but it may help reduce the risk of coinfection with human and avian viruses.
Original source text (English)
The Oregon Health Authority on Nov. 19 confirmed the state's first human case this year of highly pathogenic avian influenza linked to a cattle outbreak at a Morrow County dairy farm. The person was treated with an antiviral medicine, oseltamivir, after they developed mild symptoms of HPAI, OHA said in a news release. They were not hospitalized. The virus is not known to spread from human to human. The risk to the public is "extremely low," OHA said. Epidemiologists at OHA received confirmation of the case from the Oregon State Public Health Laboratory on Nov. 18. Oregon public health officials monitored more than 300 people exposed to sick animals since 2022, including one human case of bird flu identified in 2024 in connection with a commercial poultry outbreak, OHA said. Epidemiologists are working with the Oregon Department of Agriculture, which provides personal protection equipment and training to the affected farm workers, and Morrow County Public Health, who are monitoring those exposed to the virus, OHA said. Public health officials offered oseltamivir to those in the person's household out of caution, OHA said. "This is not cause for alarm. Rather, it is evidence that the surveillance and response system we have in place is working," Melissa Sutton, medical director for respiratory viral pathogens at OHA, said. "Our goal is to ensure that all symptomatic farm workers receive prompt testing and treatment to prevent severe disease." The seasonal flu vaccine does not protect against HPAI. Getting the vaccine can help reduce the risk of getting sick with human influenza viruses, which can reduce the risk of coinfection with both human and seasonal virus and an avian virus, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. Oregon confirms first human case of bird flu this year
新闻简报俄勒冈州确认了今年首例人感染禽流感病例,与莫罗县农场奶牛中的 暴发 (HPAI) 病毒有关。受影响的个人身份尚未确定,他出现了轻微症状并得到了及时治疗,其家庭成员也接受了预防性 治疗。无需住院治疗,俄勒冈州卫生局官员称公众面临的风险“极低”。目前还没有出现人传人的 传播 病例,巴氏杀菌牛奶和乳制品仍然可以安全食用。该确认凸显了 监测 系统的有效性,尽管该案件与 HPAI 暴发 之间的确切联系仍在调查中。官员们强调监测和治疗有症状的农场工人以预防严重疾病的重要性。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Oregon confirms first human case of bird flu this year

News Brief
Oregon has confirmed its first human case of bird flu this year, linked to an outbreak of highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) in dairy cattle at a Morrow County farm. The affected individual, who has not been identified, developed mild symptoms and was treated promptly, with members of their household also receiving precautionary treatment. No hospitalizations were required, and the risk to the public is described as "extremely low" by Oregon Health Authority officials. There have been no cases of human-to-human transmission, and pasteurized milk and dairy products remain safe for consumption. The confirmation highlights the effectiveness of the surveillance system, though the exact connection between the case and the HPAI outbreak is still under investigation. Officials emphasize the importance of monitoring and treating symptomatic farm workers to prevent severe disease.
Original source text (English)
State health officials confirmed the state's first human case of bird flu this year. It's linked to an outbreak of the highly pathogenic avian influenza (HPAI) in dairy cattle at a Morrow County farm. The person, who's not been identified, started to develop mild symptoms of the virus and were quickly treated, Oregon Health Authority officials said. They didn't need to be hospitalized, and members of their household were also given treatment as a precaution. Epidemiologists with OHA's Public Health Division confirmed the case on Nov. 18 — the first case in a human reported in Oregon since 2024. In total, OHA has only documented around 300 people being exposed to sick animals over the last four years. It's unclear if this case was related to the recent discovery of bird flu in a commercial dairy farm in Morrow County. That case was the first time the disease was detected in Oregon this year and has reached herds in 22 states. Pasteurized milk and dairy products are still safe to consume and there is no concern about the safety of the commercial milk supply. Pasteurization effectively kills the virus, and milk from sick animals is prohibited from entering the commercial supply, officials said. Washington state has dealt with more frequent cases. One person in Yakima County recently died from bird flu complications after contracting it from infected cattle. However, officials confirmed that they had underlying health conditions which made them more vulnerable. Despite the virus's expensive impact on America's poultry industry over the last few years, there haven't been any cases of human-to-human transmission. Risk to the public is still "extremely low," OHA said. "This is not cause for alarm. Rather, it is evidence that the surveillance and response system we have in place is working," said Melissa Sutton, M.D., medical director for respiratory viral pathogens at OHA. "Our goal is to ensure that all symptomatic farm workers receive prompt testing and treatment to prevent severe disease. We appreciate the partnership with individuals on the farm during this challenging time," Sutton concluded.
